## gurafu

`gurafu` 0.1.0 · needs: —

gurafu (グラフ) — directed graphs as values: build them, ask them, order them.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gurafu::ordered(ns)` |  |  |  | ordered: the distinct nodes of a list, sorted under compare. |
| `gurafu::edge_ok(e)` |  |  |  | edge_ok: an edge when it is a pair of nodes, else a :bad_option raise. |
| `gurafu::graph(edges, opts)` |  |  |  | graph: a graph from edge pairs; `nodes:` adds nodes with no edge, and `directed: false` adds each edge both ways. |
| `gurafu::nodes(g)` |  |  |  | nodes: every node of a graph, sorted. |
| `gurafu::neighbors(g, node)` |  |  |  | neighbors: the nodes one edge away from a node, sorted; [] for a node the graph does not hold. |
| `gurafu::edges(g)` |  |  |  | edges: every edge of a graph as a [from, to] pair, sorted. |
| `gurafu::has_edge?(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | has_edge?: whether a graph holds the edge from one node to another. |
| `gurafu::add_node(g, node)` |  |  |  | add_node: a graph that also holds a node. |
| `gurafu::add_edge(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | add_edge: a graph that also holds the edge from one node to another, adding both nodes. |
| `gurafu::remove_edge(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | remove_edge: a graph without one edge; its nodes stay. |
| `gurafu::remove_node(g, node)` |  |  |  | remove_node: a graph without a node and every edge at it. |
| `gurafu::reverse(g)` |  | yes |  | reverse: a graph with every edge turned around. |
| `gurafu::subgraph(g, keep)` |  |  |  | subgraph: a graph restricted to some nodes and the edges between them. |
| `gurafu::leaves(g)` |  |  |  | leaves: the nodes no edge leaves, sorted. |
| `gurafu::spread(g, frontier, seen)` |  |  |  | spread: the nodes found by following edges out of a frontier, given those already seen. |
| `gurafu::reachable(g, start)` |  |  |  | reachable: every node a start node reaches by one edge or more, sorted; the start is in it only when it lies on a cycle. |
| `gurafu::in_degrees(g)` |  |  |  | in_degrees: for each node, how many edges reach it. |
| `gurafu::peel(g, indeg, ready, done)` |  |  |  | peel: the layers of a graph from a ready layer on, each node placed once every edge into it is placed. |
| `gurafu::layers(g)` |  |  |  | layers: the acyclic part of a graph in layers: sources first, then each node one layer past its deepest upstream, each layer sorted. |
| `gurafu::back_walk(back, path, seen)` |  |  |  | back_walk: a cycle found by following predecessors among nodes that all have one, from the path walked so far. |
| `gurafu::cycle(g)` |  | yes |  | cycle: one cycle of a graph as its nodes in edge order, starting at the smallest, or nil when the graph has none. |
| `gurafu::cycle_text(c)` |  |  |  | cycle_text: a cycle written as its path back to where it starts. |
| `gurafu::topo(g)` |  |  |  | topo: every node ordered so each edge points forward, in layers from the sources, each layer sorted; a cycle is refused with :refused, naming it. |
| `gurafu::depth(g, node)` |  |  |  | depth: how many edges lie on the longest path from a source to a node; a cycle is refused as topo refuses it. |
| `gurafu::random_edges(seed, nodes_count, n, forward)` |  |  |  | random_edges: n edges over nodes 0 to size - 1 drawn from a seed; with `forward: true` each edge points from a smaller node to a larger one. |
| `gurafu::respects?(g, order)` |  |  |  | respects?: whether an order puts the start of every edge of a graph before its end. |
| `gurafu::real_cycle?(g, c)` |  |  |  | real_cycle?: whether a list of nodes is a cycle of a graph: each one has an edge to the next, and the last to the first. |
| `gurafu::acyclic?(g)` |  |  |  | acyclic?: whether no node of a graph reaches itself, judged by reachable alone. |

## happa

`happa` 0.1.0 · needs: —

happa (葉っぱ) — a template's leaves: split, graph, change, schedule and verdicts, as pure decisions.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `happa::text_of(cs, from, to)` |  |  |  | text_of: the characters from one index up to another, joined. |
| `happa::name_end(cs, i)` |  |  |  | name_end: the first index at or after i that holds a dot or an opening bracket, or the end. |
| `happa::quoted(a, cs, i, buf)` |  |  |  | quoted: a quoted index key read from i to its closing quote, with the index after the closing bracket. |
| `happa::bare(a, cs, i)` |  |  |  | bare: an unquoted index key read from i to its closing bracket, with the index after it. |
| `happa::steps_from(a, cs, i, acc)` |  |  |  | steps_from: the steps of an address from index i on, each a name and its index key or nil. |
| `happa::steps(address)` |  |  |  | steps: an address read into its steps; a malformed address raises :parse_error, never a guess. |
| `happa::key(address)` |  |  |  | key: a resource address's leaf key: its first index key, else its top module, else the address itself. |
| `happa::strip_index(address)` |  |  |  | strip_index: an address with every index key dropped. |
| `happa::data?(address)` |  |  |  | data?: whether an address names a data source, at the top or inside a module. |
| `happa::key_id(k)` |  |  |  | key_id: text that orders leaf keys as the operator does: keyed, then module, then address, then by value. |
| `happa::managed(addresses)` |  |  |  | managed: the addresses that are not data sources. |
| `happa::classify(addresses, p95_ms, budget_ms)` |  |  |  | classify: whether a state is fat: more than one leaf key, or a cycle p95 over its budget. |
| `happa::root_of(parent, id)` |  |  |  | root_of: the root a key's chain of parents ends at. |
| `happa::joined(parent, edge)` |  |  |  | joined: the parent map after one reference edge joins two leaves, the smaller key becoming the root. |
| `happa::split(addresses, refs)` |  | yes |  | split: the leaves of a state: its managed addresses grouped by leaf key, then joined along reference edges between keys; a repeated address is refused. |
| `happa::le64(n)` |  |  |  | le64: a count as its eight little-endian bytes. |
| `happa::multiset_hash(addresses)` |  |  |  | multiset_hash: the BLAKE3 of a list of addresses taken as a multiset: the count, then each address sorted with its length, so order never matters and no two lists concatenate alike. |
| `happa::bijection(before, leaves)` |  |  |  | bijection: whether the leaves hold exactly the managed addresses of the state before the split: equal multiset hashes and no address in two leaves. |
| `happa::alnum?(c)` |  |  |  | alnum?: whether a character is an ASCII letter or digit. |
| `happa::slug(raw)` |  |  |  | slug: a key as a DNS label part: ASCII letters and digits lowercased, every other run one dash, no dash at either end, and "leaf" when nothing is left. |
| `happa::suffix(k)` |  |  |  | suffix: eight hex characters of the BLAKE3 of a key's kind and value, that tell apart keys whose slugs collide. |
| `happa::name_cap` |  |  |  | name_cap: the longest object name a leaf may have, a DNS label's 63. |
| `happa::names(parent, keys)` |  |  |  | names: the object name of each leaf key under a parent, in order: parent and slug, with a hash suffix when two keys share a slug or the name is too long. |
| `happa::name(parent, k, keys)` |  |  |  | name: the object name of one leaf key among the keys of its parent. |
| `happa::canonical(v)` |  |  |  | canonical: a value as canonical JSON text: object keys sorted, a comma after every member, so equal values give equal text. |
| `happa::digest(catalog, own, refs)` |  |  |  | digest: the content hash of a leaf's inputs: the catalog's variables, the leaf's own and its references, each canonical, nil marking one absent. |
| `happa::changed(current, recorded)` |  |  |  | changed: the leaves whose recorded digest differs from the current one, sorted; a leaf with no recorded digest has not changed. |
| `happa::graph(leaves, refs)` |  |  |  | graph: the leaf graph from leaf names and [leaf, upstream] references, each edge pointing from the upstream to the leaf; a cycle is refused with :refused, naming the cycle and every leaf it holds. |
| `happa::order(g)` |  |  |  | order: the apply order of a leaf graph, every leaf after its upstreams, in layers, each sorted by name. |
| `happa::destroy_order(g)` |  |  |  | destroy_order: the reverse of the apply order: dependents before their upstreams. |
| `happa::upstreams(g, leaf)` |  |  |  | upstreams: the leaves a leaf reads from, sorted. |
| `happa::dependents(g, held)` |  |  |  | dependents: every leaf that reads, directly or through others, from any of some leaves, sorted. |
| `happa::blocked(ups, ready, applying)` |  |  |  | blocked: which upstreams hold a leaf back: those not ready, where a mocked output lets a plan through but never an apply; sorted, each named once. |
| `happa::classes` |  |  |  | classes: the work classes in priority order: an approved apply, a changed leaf's plan, a leaf whose upstream became ready, a retry, a drift plan. |
| `happa::ranked(candidates, priority)` |  |  |  | ranked: candidates in the order they are offered a slot: by class, drift oldest first, then upstreams first by depth, then by name; with priority off, by arrival then name. |
| `happa::drift_slots(scope_cap, share_permille)` |  |  |  | drift_slots: how many of a scope's slots drift may hold: its share of the cap, at least one and, past a cap of one, never all; none when the share is zero. |
| `happa::bump(m, k)` |  |  |  | bump: a count map with one key raised by one. |
| `happa::fits?(c, limits, f)` |  |  |  | fits?: whether one more candidate fits the scope cap, the drift reserve and the per-credential cap, given what is in flight. |
| `happa::admit(candidates, limits, in_flight)` |  |  |  | admit: who runs next: candidates offered slots in ranked order under the global cap, each scope's cap, the drift reserve and the per-credential cap, given the work in flight; the names admitted, in order. |
| `happa::offsets(names, interval)` |  |  |  | offsets: each leaf's place in a drift interval: the leaves ranked by the BLAKE3 of their names and spread evenly across it, so no stretch of the interval holds more than its share. |
| `happa::last_slot(offset, now, interval)` |  |  |  | last_slot: the start of a leaf's most recent drift slot at a moment, or nil before its first slot. |
| `happa::drift_due(leaves, now, interval)` |  |  |  | drift_due: the leaves whose drift slot has come since their last check, sorted by name; a leaf never checked is due from its first slot. |
| `happa::skip_reasons` |  |  |  | skip_reasons: the typed world facts that let a fat template run as one, the annotation's values. |
| `happa::skip_annotation` |  |  |  | skip_annotation: the annotation a template declares its skip reason in. |
| `happa::skip_reason(raw)` |  |  |  | skip_reason: the typed reason an annotation's value names before any colon, or nil when it names none. |
| `happa::leaves_path(workspace)` |  |  |  | leaves_path: the leaf declaration a workspace's leaves are written in. |
| `happa::admission(classification, skip, mode, never_reconciled, fix?)` |  |  |  | admission: whether a template is admitted: a leaf always; a fat one when admission is off, when it declares a typed skip reason, or when it already reconciled; a new fat one is refused naming its workspace's leaf declaration, and under :generate with a draft entry to commit. |
| `happa::ceil_div(a, b)` |  |  |  | ceil_div: whole-number division rounded up. |
| `happa::p95(samples)` |  |  |  | p95: the nearest-rank 95th percentile of the newest 32 cycle times, or nil when there are none. |
| `happa::bucket_up(ms)` |  |  |  | bucket_up: a duration rounded up to the next whole second, so jitter under a second never moves the floor. |
| `happa::without_nil(m)` |  |  |  | without_nil: a record without the fields whose value is nil, as a status field omits them. |
| `happa::ratchet(prev, leaf_keys, fat, p95_ms, tolerance)` |  |  |  | ratchet: a template's next `status.leaf`: the leaf key count and cycle p95 measured, the floor that may only fall, and every regression past it (the p95 within a tolerance in percent). |
| `happa::stall_after_ms` |  |  |  | stall_after_ms: how long a planning or applying leaf may go without a heartbeat before it is stalled, unless the leaf says otherwise. |
| `happa::health(leaf, now_ms)` |  |  |  | health: one leaf's verdict and alert verdict at a moment: rate limited until a time, failed, stalled when its heartbeat is older than its stall window, else its phase; failed and stalled leaves fire an alert. |
| `happa::holding?(state)` |  |  |  | holding?: whether a leaf state holds its dependents back: failed, stalled or rate limited. |
| `happa::counted(xs, cap)` |  |  |  | counted: a status list capped at a number of names, with how many were left out. |
| `happa::judge(leaves, cap)` |  |  |  | judge: the parent's partial state over its leaves: Partial when any leaf failed, stalled or is blocked by one that holds it, Converged when every leaf is ready, else Progressing; counts per state, and the held and blocked leaves named, up to a cap per list. |
| `happa::slice(rendered, addresses)` |  |  |  | slice: a leaf's Terraform JSON cut from the whole render: only the resources and modules its addresses name, every other block kept whole. |
| `happa::inputs(declaration, values)` |  |  |  | inputs: each leaf's input slice in the keyed form: the shared inputs and the collection cut to the one entry the leaf's key names; an entry with no key, or a key held twice, is refused. |
| `happa::share_ok(v)` |  |  |  | share_ok: a check that a value is a fraction from zero up to, not including, one. |
| `happa::count_ok(lo)` |  |  |  | count_ok: a check that a value is a whole number of at least a floor. |
| `happa::cap_schema` |  |  |  | cap_schema: each leaf setting's fields: a cap, a share, a mode, the rate budget and the queue. |
| `happa::cap_defaults` |  |  |  | cap_defaults: the value each leaf setting takes when nothing declares it. |
| `happa::cap_offs` |  |  |  | cap_offs: the value each leaf setting takes when it is configured off: the monolithic or unpaced form, never a deletion. |
| `happa::setting(kind, fields)` |  |  |  | setting: one leaf setting's entry: its kind is the setting, its name says where it came from. |
| `happa::caps(declaration)` |  |  |  | caps: the leaf settings of §9 from a declaration of entries: each judged alone, a bad entry refused and its default kept, an entry configured off taking its off value; then the cross-entry rules, each refusing only its own entry. |
| `happa::same?(a, b)` |  |  |  | same?: whether two values render as the same JSON, the form the operator's answers are recorded in. |
| `happa::as_key(k)` |  |  |  | as_key: a recorded leaf key read back as happa writes it. |

`heni` 0.1.1 · needs: hikisuu, retsu, moji, shisutemu, deeta

heni (変異) — mutation testing for a blue package: each mutation changes one literal in a fresh copy of the package, runs its tests, and must make them fail.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `heni::hn_apply(text, find, replace)` | `heni::apply` | yes | `B0013` | hn_apply: {text} with `find` replaced, or {error} when it does not occur exactly once. |
| `heni::verdict(applied, status)` | `hn_verdict` (old) |  |  | verdict: The verdict for one mutation from its test run's status. nil means the |
| `heni::mutations(parsed)` | `hn_mutations` (old) |  |  | mutations: Mutations from parsed JSON: [{name, file, find, replace}]. |
| `heni::package_name(dir)` | `hn_package_name` (old) |  |  | package_name: The package's name: the last segment of its directory. |
| `heni::copy(src, dest)` | `hn_copy` (old) |  |  | copy: Copy every file under `src` to `dest`, keeping relative paths. |
| `heni::fresh(pkg_dir, tmp, tag)` | `hn_fresh` (old) |  |  | fresh: A fresh root holding one copy of the package: <tmp>/heni-<now>-<tag>/<name>. |
| `heni::hn_test(blue, root, name, test_file)` | `heni::test` |  | `B0013` | hn_test: Run the copy's tests with the fresh root first on BLUE_PATH. The capture. |
| `heni::status(cap)` | `hn_status` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::run(spec)` | `hn_run` (old) |  |  | run: spec: {package, mutations, blue, test, tmp}. Returns the report. |
| `heni::one(spec, name, test_file, i, m)` | `hn_one` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::report_text(r)` | `hn_report_text` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::detail(x)` | `hn_detail` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::ok?(r)` | `hn_ok?` (old) |  |  | ok?: whether every mutation was caught. |
| `heni::cli` | `hn_cli` (old) |  |  | cli: heni's command line: the package and its mutations file, and which blue and which test file to run. |
| `heni::main` | `hn_main` (old) |  |  | main: the command's entry point: argv read by heni's cli through hikisuu, which answers help and completion and refuses a bad line with exit 64. |
| `heni::mutate(a)` | `hn_mutate` (old) |  |  | mutate: one heni run from a parsed command line: every mutation run, the report on stdout, and an error when the run is not clean. |
| `heni::end_to_end` | `hn_end_to_end` (old) |  |  |  |

## hikisuu

`hikisuu` 0.1.0 · needs: jisho, kyohi, moji, retsu

hikisuu (引数) — command-line arguments: one declared spec is parsed into a record, rendered as help, and answered as shell completion.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hikisuu::flag_text(key)` |  |  |  | flag_text: the spelling of a flag key on the command line. |
| `hikisuu::kind_of(default)` |  |  |  | kind_of: the kind a flag default implies: switch, int or text. |
| `hikisuu::flag_record(key, entry)` |  |  |  | flag_record: a flag entry as one record of key, text, kind, default, about, min and value. |
| `hikisuu::flags_of(flags)` |  |  |  | flags_of: the flag records a flags map declares, in key order. |
| `hikisuu::slot_record(entry)` |  |  |  | slot_record: a positional entry as one record of name, about, many and optional. |
| `hikisuu::command_record(entry)` |  |  |  | command_record: a command entry, a bare name or a record, as one record of name, about, flags and positional. |
| `hikisuu::commands_of(spec)` |  |  |  | commands_of: the command records a spec declares; empty when it has none. |
| `hikisuu::shape(spec, command)` |  |  |  | shape: the flags and positionals in force for a command of a spec, nil for none, refusing a key declared twice. |
| `hikisuu::help_wanted?(args)` |  |  |  | help_wanted?: whether a line asks for help before any `--`. |
| `hikisuu::read_value(f, raw)` |  |  |  | read_value: a flag's text value read as its kind, with the fault named when it does not read. |
| `hikisuu::split_flag(arg)` |  |  |  | split_flag: a `--name=value` argument as its name and value; value nil without `=`. |
| `hikisuu::walk(args, flags, acc)` |  |  |  | walk: the flags, positional words and problems a line holds, read left to right. |
| `hikisuu::problem(acc, text)` |  |  |  | problem: an accumulator with one more problem. |
| `hikisuu::value(acc, key, v)` |  |  |  | value: an accumulator with one flag's value set. |
| `hikisuu::place(words, slots, values, problems)` |  |  |  | place: positional words bound to their slots, with a problem for each missing or extra word. |
| `hikisuu::slot_label(s)` |  |  |  | slot_label: how help and errors name a positional: upper case, with dots when it takes many. |
| `hikisuu::command_names(spec)` |  |  |  | command_names: the names of a spec's commands joined for a sentence. |
| `hikisuu::pick_command(args, spec)` |  |  |  | pick_command: the command a line names and the arguments after it, or a problem. |
| `hikisuu::sound(spec)` |  |  |  | sound: a spec checked whole, its own flags and every command's, before any line is read; returns the spec. |
| `hikisuu::parse(args, spec)` |  |  |  | parse: a command line read by a spec into one record of every flag, positional and the command; every fault raised together as `:usage`. |
| `hikisuu::usage_line(spec, command)` |  |  |  | usage_line: the one-line synopsis of a spec, or of one of its commands. |
| `hikisuu::flag_left(f)` |  |  |  | flag_left: a flag's left column in help: its text, and its value word when it takes one. |
| `hikisuu::flag_right(f)` |  |  |  | flag_right: a flag's right column in help: its about text and a default worth showing. |
| `hikisuu::columns(heading, rows)` |  |  |  | columns: rows of a left and right text laid out as two aligned columns under a heading. |
| `hikisuu::render(spec, command)` |  |  |  | render: the help text of a spec, or of one of its commands. |
| `hikisuu::complete(args, spec)` |  |  |  | complete: the words that may follow a partial command line, for the last word as typed so far. |
| `hikisuu::matching(typed, candidates)` |  |  |  | matching: the candidates that start with what was typed, sorted. |
| `hikisuu::script(spec, shell)` |  |  |  | script: the completion script a shell sources for a program, asking the program itself through `__complete`. |
| `hikisuu::outcome(args, spec)` |  |  |  | outcome: what a command line asks of a program: help text, completion words, a usage refusal with its exit status, or the record to run with. |
| `hikisuu::run_with(args, spec, f, io)` |  |  |  | run_with: one program run through its spec against given sinks for stdout, stderr and exit; returns what the program returned, or nil. |
| `hikisuu::command(spec, f)` |  |  |  | command: the program's door: argv read by the spec; help and completion printed, a bad line refused on stderr with exit 64, otherwise f called with the record. |
| `hikisuu::souji_like` |  |  |  | souji_like: a spec shaped like souji's, the fixture the tests read. |

## hon

`hon` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, moji, mokuroku, retsu, shisutemu

hon (本) — a project's book: its pages, each of one Diátaxis kind, rendered into the files a reader or an agent opens.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hon::page_kinds` |  |  |  | page_kinds: The four Diátaxis kinds, in the order a book shows them. |
| `hon::front_matter_lines(text)` |  |  |  | front_matter_lines: The lines between a page's opening `---` and the next `---`, or [] when the page has none. |
| `hon::front_matter(text)` |  |  |  | front_matter: A page's front matter as a map from each key to its text. |
| `hon::front_matter_text(kind, summary)` |  |  |  | front_matter_text: The front matter block for a page of `kind` with a one-line `summary`, ending in a blank line. |
| `hon::front_matter_of(pairs)` |  |  |  | front_matter_of: A front matter block from [key, value] pairs, in order, ending in a blank line. |
| `hon::page_body(text)` |  |  |  | page_body: A page without its front matter. |
| `hon::page_kind(text)` |  |  |  | page_kind: The kind a page's front matter declares, as a keyword, or nil when it declares none the book knows. |
| `hon::kind_refusals(pages)` |  |  |  | kind_refusals: One refusal for each page, given as [path, text], that declares no kind or a kind the book does not know. |
| `hon::doc_pages(root)` |  |  |  | doc_pages: Every Markdown page under `root/docs`, as [path from root, text], sorted by path. |
| `hon::normal_path(path)` |  |  |  | normal_path: A relative path with each `.` step and each `dir/..` pair removed. |
| `hon::common_steps(a, b)` |  |  |  | common_steps: How many leading steps two split paths share. |
| `hon::relative_path(from, path)` |  |  |  | relative_path: The path to `path` as a file in directory `from` writes it, both paths relative to one root. |
| `hon::path_in(dir, target)` |  |  |  | path_in: The root-relative path of `target` written in a file in directory `dir`. |
| `hon::outside_link?(target)` |  |  |  | outside_link?: Whether a link target leaves the project: a URL, a mail address, an anchor or an absolute path. |
| `hon::rebased_target(target, from, to)` |  |  |  | rebased_target: A link target written in directory `from`, rewritten to reach the same file from directory `to`. |
| `hon::rebased_links(text, from, to)` |  |  |  | rebased_links: Markdown text moved from directory `from` to directory `to`, each relative link rewritten to reach the same file. |
| `hon::fence_line?(line)` |  |  |  | fence_line?: Whether a line opens or closes a fenced code block. |
| `hon::outside_fences(text, f)` |  |  |  | outside_fences: The text with `f` applied to every line that is not inside a fenced code block. |
| `hon::demoted(text, n)` |  |  |  | demoted: The text with every heading outside code moved `n` levels down. |
| `hon::page_title(body, fallback)` |  |  |  | page_title: The text of a page body's first `# ` heading, or `fallback` when it has none. |
| `hon::without_title(body)` |  |  |  | without_title: A page body without its `# ` heading and the blank lines around it. |
| `hon::lead_sentence(body)` |  |  |  | lead_sentence: The first line of prose in a page body, used when its front matter gives no summary. |
| `hon::lead_and_sections(body)` |  |  |  | lead_and_sections: [lead, sections] of a page body: the text before its first `## ` heading, and the text from it on. |
| `hon::page_of(path, text)` |  |  |  | page_of: A page's record from its path and text: path, kind, title, summary, order, role, runs and body. |
| `hon::kind_rank(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_rank: Where a kind stands in the book's order, from 0. |
| `hon::book_order(pages)` |  |  |  | book_order: Pages in the book's order: by kind, then by their `order`, then by path. |
| `hon::kind_heading(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_heading: The heading a book gives the part of one kind. |
| `hon::project_of(pages, manifest)` |  |  |  | project_of: A project record from its pages, given as [path, text], and the manifest of its Bluefile.lock. |
| `hon::project_at(root)` |  |  |  | project_at: The project whose root is `root`: its pages under `docs/` and its Bluefile.lock manifest. |
| `hon::generated_outputs(project)` |  |  |  | generated_outputs: The paths the project's Bluefile declares as generated files. |
| `hon::page_with_role(project, role)` |  |  |  | page_with_role: The project's page whose front matter names `role`, or nil. |
| `hon::listed_pages(project, kind)` |  |  |  | listed_pages: The pages a book lists under `kind`: every page of that kind except the introduction and the book itself. |
| `hon::rendered_path(page)` |  |  |  | rendered_path: Where a reader finds a page: the agents page is read as the AGENTS.md it renders, every other page at its own path. |
| `hon::page_link(page, to)` |  |  |  | page_link: A list item linking to a page from directory `to`, with its summary. |
| `hon::book_map(project, level, to)` |  |  |  | book_map: Every listed page grouped by kind under headings of `level`, each linked from directory `to`. |
| `hon::rendered_notice(sources)` |  |  |  | rendered_notice: The HTML comment that opens a rendered file and names its sources and the command that rewrites it. |
| `hon::page_body_at(page, to)` |  |  |  | page_body_at: A page's body with its links rewritten for a file in directory `to`. |
| `hon::paragraphs(parts)` |  |  |  | paragraphs: Text joined from parts, one blank line between each and a line break at the end. |
| `hon::readme_text(project)` |  |  |  | readme_text: README.md: the introduction's lead, a quickstart from the first tutorial, the introduction's sections, and a map of the book. |
| `hon::build_section(project)` |  |  |  | build_section: How to build and test the project, read from its Bluefile.lock: the checks, the generated files and the commands. |
| `hon::book_line(project)` |  |  |  | book_line: The sentence that names the project's book, or empty text when it has none. |
| `hon::book_pointer(project)` |  |  |  | book_pointer: Where a reader finds the book and the index of every page, as one paragraph: an agent file stays short and points into the book. |
| `hon::agents_text(project)` |  |  |  | agents_text: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md: the agents page's lead, the build commands from the Bluefile, the page's sections, and where the book is. |
| `hon::llms_text(project)` |  |  |  | llms_text: llms.txt: the project's name as the heading, its summary as a quote, then one section of links per kind, explanation last as Optional. |
| `hon::llms_full_text(project)` |  |  |  | llms_full_text: llms-full.txt: the introduction, then the full text of every hand-written tutorial, how-to and reference page, each opened by its path. |
| `hon::doc_parts(name, lines)` |  |  |  | doc_parts: [prose, examples] of a definition's `@doc` lines: the prose with the leading `name: ` removed, and each indented example line without its indent. |
| `hon::entry_section(pkg, entry)` |  |  |  | entry_section: The reference section for one definition of package `pkg`: its qualified signature, its doc prose and its examples as a blue block. |
| `hon::reference_text(title, records)` |  |  |  | reference_text: The reference of every package in `records` (mokuroku's catalogue records): each package's purpose, then each definition's signature, doc and examples. |
| `hon::heading_anchor(text)` |  |  |  | heading_anchor: The anchor GitHub gives a heading: lower case, spaces as hyphens, other punctuation dropped. |
| `hon::link_from(to, target)` |  |  |  | link_from: A link to `target` written in directory `to`: a URL as it is, a project path made relative. |
| `hon::reference_links(builtins, records, to, reference, catalog)` |  |  |  | reference_links: A map from each name a reader may write in code to the place the reference documents it: a built-in, given as [name, topic title], under its topic in `reference`; a package, and each of its definitions, under the package in `catalog`. Paths are written from directory `to`. |
| `hon::called_name(span)` |  |  |  | called_name: The name a code span calls, when the span is a call: the text before its first parenthesis. |
| `hon::linked_code(line, links)` |  |  |  | linked_code: A line with each inline code span that names an entry of `links` turned into a link to it; a span already inside a link is left alone. |
| `hon::chapters(project)` |  |  |  | chapters: The book's numbered chapters: every listed tutorial, then every listed how-to, each as [number, page]. |
| `hon::chapter_heading(chapter)` |  |  |  | chapter_heading: A chapter's heading text: its number and its title. |
| `hon::book_contents(project)` |  |  |  | book_contents: The book's table of contents: each part with its chapters linked to their anchors, and the reference and explanation pages linked from directory `to`. |
| `hon::chapter_text(chapter, links, to)` |  |  |  | chapter_text: One chapter as the book prints it: a numbered heading, then the page body with its headings moved under it, its links rewritten for directory `to`, and its code names linked to the reference. |
| `hon::book_text(project, links, to)` |  |  |  | book_text: The book in Markdown, written to a file in directory `to`: its contents, the tutorial and how-to chapters in full, and the reference and explanation pages as linked lists. |
| `hon::fenced_blocks(body)` |  |  |  | fenced_blocks: Every fenced code block in a page body, as a record of its language, its arguments and its code. |
| `hon::block_arg(args, key)` |  |  |  | block_arg: The value of argument `key=value` among a block's arguments, or nil. |
| `hon::command_words(line)` |  |  |  | command_words: The words of a console command, with single and double quotes grouping words as a shell reads them. |
| `hon::console_steps(code)` |  |  |  | console_steps: The commands of a console block, each as [command line, expected output lines]. |
| `hon::runnable_page?(page)` |  |  |  | runnable_page?: Whether the docs check runs a page's blocks: every tutorial and how-to, and any page whose front matter says `runs: true`. |
| `hon::run_command(blue, dir, words)` |  |  |  | run_command: Run one console command in directory `dir` with blue at `blue`: leading NAME=value words set the environment, and a leading `blue` is the blue under test. |
| `hon::trimmed_output(text)` |  |  |  | trimmed_output: Command output without its trailing line breaks, as the book shows it. |
| `hon::step_failure(line, expected, cap, failing)` |  |  |  | step_failure: Why one console step does not hold, or nil. A step holds when its command exits 0 and, if the page shows output, prints exactly it; in a block marked `fails`, when its command exits non-zero and prints every line the page shows. |
| `hon::run_console(blue, root, here, steps, failing)` |  |  |  | run_console: Run a console block's steps in the sandbox `root`, from the directory `here` inside it; returns [here, failures]. `cd` moves `here`; every other step is judged by `step_failure`. |
| `hon::inspect_text(text)` |  |  |  | inspect_text: Text shown in a failure message as the list of its lines, so a line break or a trailing space is visible. |
| `hon::write_block_file(path, code)` |  |  |  | write_block_file: Write a block's code to `path`: a blue file through the formatter, any other file as written. |
| `hon::run_blocks(blue, scratch, page)` |  |  |  | run_blocks: Run every blue and console block of one page, in order, in a fresh directory under `scratch`; returns the failures, each naming the page. A block with `file=PATH` is written there; a blue block without one runs, with `blue test` when it holds a test; a console block with the argument `fails` must fail; a console block with the argument `host` needs the network or nix and is shown, not run. |
| `hon::builtin_topics(reference)` |  |  |  | builtin_topics: Each built-in name with the title of its topic, as [name, title], from the JSON `blue reference` prints. |
| `hon::published_reference(project)` |  |  |  | published_reference: Where the language reference is read: the project's own `docs/REFERENCE.md` when it has one, else blue's published copy. |
| `hon::book_links(blue, root, project, to)` |  |  |  | book_links: The code names a book written in directory `to` links: every built-in, from the reference that `blue` at `blue` prints, and every definition of the project's own packages under `root`. |
| `hon::rendering(name, root, to)` |  |  |  | rendering: The text of one rendering of the project at `root`, by name: `:readme`, `:agents`, `:llms`, `:llms_full`, or `:book` for a book written in directory `to`. |
| `hon::write_rendering(name, out)` |  |  |  | write_rendering: Write one rendering of the project at $GEN_ROOT (default `.`) to $GEN_OUT (default `out`): what a Bluefile `generate` runs. |
| `hon::sample_pages` |  |  |  | sample_pages: The pages of a small sample project the tests render: an introduction, a tutorial, a how-to, an agents page and an explanation. |
| `hon::sample_project` |  |  |  | sample_project: The sample pages as a project, with a manifest that declares a catalogue, a check and a generated README. |

| `kansuu::pipe3(f, g, h)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kansuu::pipe_all(fs)` |  |  |  | pipe_all folds a LIST of functions into one, left to right. |
| `kansuu::compose_all(fs)` |  |  |  | compose_all: The mathematical order: compose_all([f, g, h]) is f∘g∘h, so h runs first. |
| `kansuu::curry2(f)` |  |  |  | curry2 turns f(a, b) into f(a)(b), so the first argument can be fixed and the |
| `kansuu::uncurry2(g)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kansuu::apply_twice(f, x)` |  |  |  | apply_twice: f(f(x)). The general form is iterate_n; this exists because doubling up is |
| `kansuu::iterated(f, n)` |  |  |  | iterated: The combinator form of iterate_n: n is fixed, the value is not. |
| `kansuu::on(op, key)` |  |  |  | on: Haskell's `on`: compare/combine two values THROUGH a key function. |
| `kansuu::juxt(fs)` |  | yes |  | juxt(fs) applies EVERY function to ONE value and collects the results. |
| `kansuu::both_results(f, g)` |  |  |  | both_results: The two-function case, named, because a pair is what most callers want and |
| `kansuu::fork(op, f, g)` |  |  |  | fork is both_results with the pair already combined: op(f(x), g(x)). |
| `kansuu::tap(f)` |  | yes |  | tap runs f for its effect and hands the ORIGINAL value on. |
| `kansuu::apply_when(p, f)` |  |  |  | apply_when: Apply f only where p holds; elsewhere pass the value through unchanged. |
| `kansuu::is_fixed_point(f, x)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kansuu::until_stable(f, x, max_steps)` |  |  |  | until_stable: Apply f until the value stops changing, or until max_steps applications. |
| `kansuu::converges(f, x, max_steps)` |  |  |  | converges: Did it actually settle within the bound? The question until_stable's return |
| `kansuu::stable_steps(f, x, max_steps)` |  |  |  | stable_steps: How many applications until stable; max_steps if it never got there. |

## kousei

`kousei` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, retsu

kousei (構成) — a system composed entry by entry: each entry checked, configured off, tuned or refused alone, then judged into what stands.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kousei::verdict(ok, why)` |  |  |  | verdict: nil when ok, else why. |
| `kousei::flag` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::text` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::one_of(choices)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::int_in(lo, hi)` |  |  |  |  |

| `koutei::escape_property(text)` |  |  |  | escape_property: an annotation property value; also escapes : and ,. |
| `koutei::levels` |  |  |  | levels: the annotation levels GitHub shows. |

| `koutei::annotate(level, message, props)` |  |  |  | annotate: raise one annotation on the step. |

## maakuappu

`maakuappu` 0.1.0 · needs: retsu, tesuto

maakuappu (マークアップ) — XML as data: a document parsed by the runtime's xml_parse (quick-xml) into element maps {name, attributes, children, text}, the queries a reader of a scanner export asks of it (children and descendants by name, attributes, text, a path of element names), and the element rendered back with every value escaped. A DTD entity is never expanded or fetched: the document is refused (:xml_refused), and a document that is not well-formed raises :xml_malformed.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `maakuappu::parse(text)` |  |  |  | parse: the root element of an XML document. Raises :xml_malformed when the text is not well-formed and :xml_refused when it declares or references a DTD entity. |
| `maakuappu::render(el)` |  |  |  | render: the element as XML text that parse reads back equal, attributes in key order and every value escaped. |
| `maakuappu::element(name, attributes, children, text)` |  |  |  | element: a new element with this name, attributes map, children and text, the shape parse returns. |
| `maakuappu::children(el, name)` |  | yes |  | children: the child elements of el named name, in document order; [] when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::child(el, name)` |  |  |  | child: the first child element of el named name, or nil. |
| `maakuappu::descendants(el, name)` |  |  |  | descendants: every element below el named name, in document order, at any depth; [] when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::attr(el, key)` |  |  |  | attr: the value of el's attribute key, or nil when el is nil or has no such attribute. |
| `maakuappu::text_of(el)` |  |  |  | text_of: el's own text with surrounding whitespace trimmed, or nil when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::find(el, path)` |  | yes |  | find: every element reached from el by following path, a list of element names, one level per name (the XPath a/b/c); [el] for the empty path, [] when el is nil. |

## raifusaikuru

`raifusaikuru` 0.1.1 · needs: retsu, shuugou, kazu

raifusaikuru (ライフサイクル) — lifecycles: event-sourced states, guards that name the rule and the task, and permit limits.

## shisei

`shisei` 0.1.0 · needs: kousei, kyohi, retsu

shisei (姿勢) — a service's posture: postures declared as kousei entries, each on by default, each configured off or tuned, each refused alone.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shisei::posture(name, table, defaults, layer)` |  |  |  | posture: one named behaviour of a service with its fields. |

| `shisei::schema(postures)` |  |  |  | schema: The kousei schema of a set of postures: each kind maps to its field table. |
| `shisei::defaults(postures)` |  |  |  | defaults: One entry per posture, running its defaults: the stance of a bare service. |
| `shisei::stance(postures, declaration)` |  |  |  | stance: The declaration laid over the defaults and judged: accepted, off and refusals. |
| `shisei::layers(postures, accepted, sink)` |  |  |  | layers: The middlewares of every accepted entry, in the order the postures are given. |
| `shisei::stack(ls, handler)` |  |  |  | stack: The first layer is outermost: it sees the request first and the response last. |
| `shisei::serving(postures, declaration, sink, handler)` |  |  |  | serving: The whole composition: the stance, its refusals and the handler it makes. |
| `shisei::gated(mode, sink, posture_name, why, deny, allow)` |  |  |  | gated: The ladder. `deny` is the refusal to send; `allow` is a function that goes on. |
| `shisei::silent` |  |  |  | silent: A sink that forgets. A sink that remembers is written where the cell is, since |
| `shisei::tag(key, value)` |  |  |  |  |
| `shisei::veto(mode_default)` |  |  |  |  |

sutoriimu (ストリーム) — NATS JetStream over the runtime's NATS words.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `sutoriimu::name_ok?(name)` |  |  |  | name_ok?: whether a stream, consumer or table name has no dot, space or wildcard. |
| `sutoriimu::need_name(what, name)` |  |  |  | need_name: a name that is ok, else a :bad_option raise naming what it names. |
| `sutoriimu::answer(text)` |  |  |  | answer: a JetStream API reply read as a record; an error reply raises :jetstream. |
| `sutoriimu::stream_config(name, subjects)` |  |  |  | stream_config: the JetStream config of a file-backed stream over subjects. |
| `sutoriimu::ensure_stream(c, name, subjects)` |  |  |  | ensure_stream: declare a stream over subjects, created when absent; its info. |
| `sutoriimu::declared(c, name, body)` |  |  |  | declared: create a stream, or update it when its name is already in use. |
| `sutoriimu::publish(c, subject, payload, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | publish: publish to a stream's subject and wait for its ack: {stream:, seq:}. |
| `sutoriimu::consumer_config(stream, name, filter, ack_wait_ms)` |  |  |  | consumer_config: the config of a durable consumer with explicit acks. |
| `sutoriimu::durable(c, stream, name, filter, ack_wait_ms)` |  |  |  | durable: declare a durable pull consumer with explicit acks on a stream. |
| `sutoriimu::deliveries(reply)` |  |  |  | deliveries: how many times JetStream has delivered a message, read from its ack subject. |
| `sutoriimu::delivered(m)` |  |  |  | delivered: a pulled message with its delivery count. |
| `sutoriimu::pull_request(batch, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | pull_request: the body of a pull for a batch, expiring after wait_ms. |
| `sutoriimu::gather(sub, batch, wait_ms, got)` |  |  |  | gather: messages read from a pull's inbox until the batch is full, a status arrives or time runs out. |
| `sutoriimu::fetch(c, stream, name, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | fetch: up to batch messages from a durable consumer, waiting at most wait_ms. |
| `sutoriimu::ack(c, m)` |  |  |  | ack: tell the stream a message is done. |
| `sutoriimu::nak(c, m, delay_ms)` |  |  |  | nak: tell the stream to deliver a message again, after delay_ms when it is not nil. |
| `sutoriimu::outbox_ddl(table)` |  |  |  | outbox_ddl: the table the outbox writes to. |
| `sutoriimu::outbox_insert(table)` |  |  |  | outbox_insert: the statement that records one event in the outbox. |
| `sutoriimu::in_transaction(pg, f)` |  |  |  | in_transaction: run f between BEGIN and COMMIT, rolling back and raising again when it raises. |
| `sutoriimu::write_with_outbox(pg, table, change, events)` |  |  |  | write_with_outbox: run a change and record its events in the same transaction; the change's rows. |
| `sutoriimu::drain_select(table, batch)` |  |  |  | drain_select: the statement that locks the next batch of outbox events. |
| `sutoriimu::drain_delete(table, ids)` |  |  |  | drain_delete: the statement that removes drained events by id. |
| `sutoriimu::drain(pg, js, table, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | drain: publish up to batch outbox events to the stream, each acked, and delete them; how many moved. |
| `sutoriimu::settle(c, handler, m)` |  |  |  | settle: run the handler on one message and ack it; nak and raise again when the handler raises. |
| `sutoriimu::work(url, stream, name, handler, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | work: a worker's loop: fetch, run the handler on each message, ack it; a raise naks and ends the worker. |
| `sutoriimu::consumer(url, stream, name, handler, opts)` |  |  |  | consumer: a supervisor running workers for a durable consumer; each worker restarted when its handler raises. |

| `teeburu::version_hash(m)` |  |  |  | version_hash: sixteen hex characters of the BLAKE3 of a model's canonical JSON, the hash a cache key and a lock pin carry. |
| `teeburu::declared_drop(to, what)` |  |  |  | declared_drop: the drop entry the new model declares for what a step removes, or nil. |
| `teeburu::drop_step(to, what)` |  |  |  | drop_step: a drop of what a new version removes, carrying the allow its drop entry declares. |
| `teeburu::key_type(m, entity)` |  |  |  | key_type: the type of an entity's key in a model, which a relation's column takes. |
| `teeburu::relation_column(rel)` |  |  |  | relation_column: the column a relation is stored in. |
| `teeburu::field_steps(name, old, new, to)` |  |  |  | field_steps: the steps between one entity's old and new declaration: renames, retypes, added and dropped fields, indexes and relations. |
| `teeburu::sources_of(spec)` |  |  |  | sources_of: the entities a new entity takes its data from: what it was renamed, split or merged from. |
| `teeburu::evolve(from, to)` |  |  |  | evolve: the typed steps from one model version to the next: add_entity, add_field, rename, retype, add_index, split, merge and drop, entities in name order; a version that does not move forward is refused. |
| `teeburu::destructive?(s)` |  |  |  | destructive?: whether a step can lose data: every drop, and a retype that narrows. |
| `teeburu::where_of(s)` |  |  |  | where_of: the place a step acts on, as text. |
| `teeburu::data_add?(s)` |  |  |  | data_add?: whether a step adds a field or an entity that a dropped one might really be, renamed. |
| `teeburu::meets?(drop, add)` |  |  |  | meets?: whether an undeclared drop and an add stand in the same place: a field dropped and one added in the same entity, or an entity dropped while another is added. |
| `teeburu::judge(steps)` |  |  |  | judge: the steps that stand and the refusals, each scoped to its step: a drop with no allow met by an add with no declared rename refuses both, and any other destructive step without `allow: :destructive` is refused alone. |
| `teeburu::ident(x)` |  |  |  | ident: a Postgres identifier, quoted. |
| `teeburu::sql_value(v)` |  |  |  | sql_value: a value as a Postgres literal, through kueri's expression renderer. |
| `teeburu::column_sql(name, rec)` |  |  |  | column_sql: one column's definition: its name, its Postgres type, NOT NULL unless nullable, and a constant default. |
| `teeburu::index_name(entity, cols)` |  |  |  | index_name: the name an index on some columns of an entity takes. |
| `teeburu::change(id, step, transaction, statements, lock_timeout)` |  |  |  | change: one migration change: its id, the step it lowers, whether it runs inside a transaction, and its statements, the lock timeout first. |
| `teeburu::keyset_loop(table, key, key_sql_type, batch, statement)` |  |  |  | keyset_loop: a batched keyset walk over a table in key order, each batch committed on its own, running a statement over that batch's keys. |
| `teeburu::pg_options(opts)` |  |  |  | pg_options: the lowering's options with their defaults: a five-second lock timeout and batches of a thousand rows. |
| `teeburu::step_id(to, s, n)` |  |  |  | step_id: the changeset id of a step: model, version, position, step and place. |
| `teeburu::pg_create(s, from, to, o, base)` |  |  |  | pg_create: the changes that create a new entity's table with its indexes and foreign keys, then copy each source's rows in keyset batches when the entity is split or merged from others. |
| `teeburu::pg_add_field(s, to, o, base)` |  |  |  | pg_add_field: the changes that add a column: one instant ADD COLUMN for a constant default; for a relation the foreign key NOT VALID then VALIDATE; for a backfill the column, a keyset backfill and, when NOT NULL, a CHECK NOT VALID, VALIDATE, SET NOT NULL; a NOT NULL column with neither default nor backfill is refused. |
| `teeburu::pg_step(s, from, to, o, n)` |  |  |  | pg_step: one judged step lowered to its Postgres changes, the fastest safe form for each: CONCURRENTLY outside a transaction, NOT VALID then VALIDATE, a constant default with no rewrite, a batched keyset backfill, lock_timeout on every change; a step Postgres cannot carry safely is refused by name. |
| `teeburu::postgres(from, to, opts)` |  |  |  | postgres: the Postgres changes for the steps the judge lets stand between two versions, in step order, with the judge's refusals beside them; a refused step never reaches the database. |
| `teeburu::xml_text(s)` |  |  |  | xml_text: text escaped for an XML element or attribute. |
| `teeburu::keifu(changes)` |  |  |  | keifu: changes as a keifu changelog, the Liquibase XML shinka applies in process: one changeSet per change, each statement its own sql element, and runInTransaction false where a change must run outside a transaction. |
| `teeburu::kv_entity(name, ent)` |  |  |  | kv_entity: one strict Valkey entity checked and given its defaults: JSON encoding, upgrade on read, strict; indexes, relations and an encoding not rendered yet are refused by name. |
| `teeburu::kv_model(name, spec)` |  |  |  | kv_model: a strict Valkey model: a versioned model whose entities each carry an encoding, a TTL, an upgrade mode and strictness, evolved by the same steps as a table. |
| `teeburu::holds?(type, v)` |  |  |  | holds?: whether a stored value is of a field type, as JSON carries it. |
| `teeburu::kv_findings(entity, rec)` |  |  |  | kv_findings: every way a stored record breaks a strict entity: not a record, a missing field, a value of the wrong type, and, unless the entity is loose, a field it does not declare. |
| `teeburu::kv_check(entity, rec)` |  |  |  | kv_check: the record when it holds to a strict entity, else one :bad_value raise naming every finding. |
| `teeburu::kv_upgrade(steps, entity)` |  |  |  | kv_upgrade: what an old stored record of an entity becomes under the next version, from the judged steps: renamed fields moved, added fields given their default, fields widened to text converted, dropped fields removed; a step Valkey cannot carry is refused by name. |
